Inability to Sustain Attention: Unable to focus on any activity for long, with attention constantly being scattered.\n  - Occupational Hyperactivity: Driving significantly more projects or work than usual in the workplace or academic setting.\n  - Somatic Delusions: The baseless conviction that there is a serious abnormality with one's body or organs.\n  - Jealous Delusions: The conviction that one's spouse or romantic partner is being unfaithful without sufficient evidence.\n  - I feel so confident and capable of absolutely anything, which makes me constantly start multiple projects and activities at once. I just feel like I never need to rest and have this unstoppable drive to keep moving.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe how you manage to complete these multiple projects and activities, or do you often find yourself jumping from one thing to another without finishing anything, and do you have difficulty sustaining focus on a single task for a prolonged period?\n\nPatient: To be honest, I almost never finish any of the projects I start because I'm constantly jumping from one thing to another. However, these episodes of high energy and elevated mood have been going on constantly for about nine months now.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any distinct periods of normal mood in between these episodes of high energy and euphoria, or has your mood been consistently unstable over the past nine months?\n\nPatient: No, my mood has been consistently unstable over the past nine months without any normal periods in between. I constantly experience rapid and unpredictable shifts, going from feeling intensely euphoric and full of energy to suddenly being extremely angry and irritable at the slightest thing.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your inability to focus and constant jumping from one task to another is directly related to your mood shifts, or do you feel like you've always had some difficulty with attention and impulsivity, even when your mood is more stable?\n\nPatient: No, I never had any issues with attention or impulsivity before all of this began. This constant task-switching and inability to focus only started about nine months ago, right alongside my high energy and mood swings. Before this period, I was always able to focus and finish what I started without any trouble at all.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about your life before these symptoms started about nine months ago - was there a particular event or change that you think might have triggered this, or did it seem to come on fairly suddenly without any clear cause?\n\nPatient: I'm really not sure of any specific trigger that could have started all of this. It just seemed to come on without any clear cause about nine months ago. Before that, my life was pretty normal and I didn't have any of these issues.\n\nDoctor: Did you experience any symptoms like these, such as excessive energy, mood swings, or difficulty focusing, during your childhood or adolescence, or have you had any previous episodes of similar symptoms before the past nine months?\n\nPatient: No, I never experienced any symptoms like this during my childhood or adolescence. This is the very first time I've ever gone through anything like this, and it all began about nine months ago."

My mind gets distracted by every little thing, making it incredibly hard to stay focused on just one task for very long. I have all this energy to start things, but sustaining my attention on them is nearly impossible.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods of significantly decreased need for sleep, such as feeling rested after only a few hours of sleep, during these episodes of high energy and euphoria, and have these episodes lasted for at least a week or been so severe that you needed hospital care?\n\nPatient: I haven't really noticed a decreased need for sleep, and I've never needed hospital care for this. However, these episodes of high energy and elevated mood have been going on constantly for about nine months now.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any distinct periods of normal mood in between these episodes of high energy and euphoria, or has your mood been consistently unstable over the past nine months?\n\nPatient: No, my mood has been consistently unstable over the past nine months without any normal periods in between. I constantly experience rapid and unpredictable shifts, going from feeling intensely euphoric and full of energy to suddenly being extremely angry and irritable at the slightest thing.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your inability to focus and constant jumping from one task to another is directly related to your mood shifts, or do you feel like you've always had some difficulty with attention and impulsivity, even when your mood is more stable?\n\nPatient: No, I never had any issues with attention or impulsivity before all of this began. This constant task-switching and inability to focus only started about nine months ago, right alongside my high energy and mood swings. Before this period, I was always able to focus and finish what I started without any trouble at all.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about your life before these symptoms started about nine months ago - was there a particular event or change that you think might have triggered this, or did it seem to come on fairly suddenly without any clear cause?\n\nPatient: I'm really not sure of any specific trigger that could have started all of this. It just seemed to come on without any clear cause about nine months ago. Before that, my life was pretty normal and I didn't have any of these issues.\n\nDoctor: Did you experience any symptoms like these, such as excessive energy, mood swings, or difficulty focusing, during your childhood or adolescence, or have you had any previous episodes of similar symptoms before the past nine months?\n\nPatient: No, I never experienced any symptoms like this during my childhood or adolescence. This is the very first time I've ever gone through anything like this, and it all began about nine months ago.\n\nPatient: No, I never experienced any symptoms like this during my childhood or adolescence. Before that, my life was pretty normal and I didn't have any of these issues.\n\nDoctor: Did you experience any symptoms like these, such as excessive energy, mood swings, or difficulty focusing, during your childhood or adolescence, or have you had any previous episodes of similar symptoms before the past nine months?\n\nPatient: No, I never experienced any symptoms like this during my childhood or adolescence. This is the very first time I've ever gone through anything like this, and it all began about nine months ago.\n\nDoctor: Considering your symptoms of high energy, mood swings, and difficulty focusing, do you think it's possible that you might be experiencing a manic episode, which is a characteristic of bipolar disorder, or do your symptoms seem more consistent with attention-deficit/hyperactivity disorder, given your inability to sustain focus and constant task-switching?\n\nPatient: Since these symptoms only started nine months ago rather than being a lifelong issue, it doesn't feel like ADHD to me. It feels much more like a major, intense episode where I also feel extremely confident, capable of anything, and incredibly social. Because of that, the idea of a manic episode makes a lot more sense to me."
  }
]
